
About DataSearch
The DataSearch feature allows you to set up and perform data searches that can span across the entire system.
You can, for example, set up DataSearch to search across all your application system files and return any
records that have the string "young" in them. For example, it does not matter whether those records are
found among the customer records, the order and order line records, purchase order records, or part and
version number records.
DataSearch sources
Before a DataSearch can be performed, you must have at least one DataSearch source set up. The DataSearch
source specifies what IDO collection is to be used for the search. You can select which IDO properties to include
in the DataSearch. You can also specify which IDO properties to display as part of the results of the DataSearch,
regardless of whether those properties were included in the search.
For example, you might want to set up a DataSearch source to search across customer records to identify
customers who have recently placed orders for bicycle seats with part numbers prefixed by "CP-".
In this case, you would not want the DataSearch to search through all the IDO properties for the customer
name, address, and contact information, because that would extend the search time. However, you would
want to display that data for all customers who have placed orders for that particular bicycle seat recently.
You would set up your DataSearch source to search through orders for which the part number includes the
"CP-" prefix and sort them by date, and then retrieve the customer information for those recent orders.
Keep in mind that each DataSearch source can include data from only one IDO collection. This means that,
if you want to search across multiple IDO collections, you must do multiple DataSearch Sources.
DataSearch source sets
In cases where you want to include data from several unrelated data sources (IDOs), you can easily create
DataSearch source sets. DataSearch source sets allow you to group several DataSearch sources into a single
set which you can then search all at the same time. This can alleviate the problem with searching across
multiple IDOs.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 234

DataSearch

•

•
Use Specified Max: Specify a maximum value in the Record Cap Value field. This is the default value.
Note: This setting is subject to any system-wide record overrides for reports that might be in effect.
•

Retrieve All: Retrieves all specified records, regardless of any other record cap settings. .
Note: This option can affect system performance if many records are retrieved.

of fields:

Order
Select the desired sort order (Ascending or Descending).
Order By
When you click Add, the expression to be used in sorting the data displays in this field.
Note: When creating an Order By expression, be mindful of these facts:
•

This clause does not determine the order in which the retrieved results are presented in the DataSearch
display. This clause determines the order in which the records are retrieved to begin with. Among
other things, this means that, if the number of records queried is greater than the record cap, an
"ascending" query does not necessarily retrieve the same set of records as a "descending" query.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 235

DataSearch

For example, searching on a customer name field using an ascending Order By clause might return
only the records of customers whose names begin with the letters A-G, whereas searching the same
field using a descending Order By clause might return only the records of customers whose names
begin with the letters Z-S.
•

9

You can override this Order By clause in the DataSearch results by using a Sort By statement at the
DataView layout level.

About DataSearch source sets
A DataSearch source set allows you to group multiple DataSearch sources together, to make it easier for users
to search multiple sources at one time.
For example, you might want to perform a DataSearch that includes records from the Customers, Customer
Orders, Customer Order Lines, and Invoices IDO collections. The easiest way to accomplish this is to create
a DataSearch source for each IDO, and then group the four sources into a DataSearch source set named
"Customer Info."
To create and maintain DataSearch Source Sets, use the DataSearch Source Sets form.

Creating a DataSearch source set
1
2
3
4
5

Initiate a new DataSearch source set record.
In the Set Name field, specify the name for the source set.
Optionally, in the Caption Override field, specify a caption to use when the source set is displayed.
In the Source Name grid, select each DataSearch source that you want to include in the set.

DataSearch
6

Save your changes.

The DataSearch source set is now available for selection on the DataSearch form and the DataSearch Source
Setup form.

About DataSearch layouts
Similar to DataView layouts, DataSearch queries can also have saved layouts. Like DataViews, you can rearrange
columns, choose to hide or show columns, and hide or show extended captions. DataSearch layouts also
display Source and Count information for each DataSearch source queried.
However, unlike DataViews, DataSearch queries can have only a single saved layout per DataSearch source
per scope level. Once saved, that layout is applied to the DataSearch that uses that source every time a user
of that scope performs the query, at least, until you delete it.
Also, DataSearch source layouts do not support summaries, calculated columns, grouping or filtering.
The search results (that is, the display of Source and Count) also have a layout, so you can choose how to
sort the results. This layout is saved with the other layouts for specific sources. The default vendor layout
displays source by count, descending, so the source with the highest count displays first.
Like predefined DataView layouts, you can view and maintain DataSearch layouts on the DataView Layouts
form.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 238

Form extensions

Chapter 12: Form extensions

About extending and replacing forms
Mongoose forms can form an inheritance hierarchy, with child forms extending parent forms in an arbitrary
tree structure. This topic introduces a variation of the extension mechanism called “extend and replace.”
When used, a form that “extends and replaces” a parent form is opened when a request to open the parent
form is received, transparent to the user. This is similar to the extend and replace feature for IDOs.
Enable this extension mechanism by selecting the Load/Replace form with extended form check box in the
User Preferences dialog box.
About the hierarchy of priorities in selecting extended forms
When the user selects this option and then requests a form, the IDO Runtime service uses this hierarchy of
priorities to determine which form definition must be returned:
1

Form Extensions form entry for Form Name
If the Form Name value is the same as the Replaced By Form value, the extend and replace feature does
not take place. The IDO Runtime service simply returns the requested form definition.

2
3

4

Descendant forms in the extension hierarchy, with the Replace Base Form property set to True
The highest descendant level
•
If there is no form found at this level, then the requested form definition is returned.
•
If there is one form found at this level, then the requested form definition is replaced by that
descendant form definition.
•
If there are multiple forms found at this level, then you must use the Form Extensions form to
determine which form definition must be returned.
Form Extensions form entry:
•
Language ID
•
Scope Type

To identify which extended form is used to replace the requested form based on the hierarchical level, select
Help > About This Form then click the Warning Message(s) icon.
How the system resolves extended form conflicts
When the system has two (or more) extended forms at the same level in the hierarchy, and both extended
forms have the "Replace Base Form" property set to True, this can result in a conflict when the form is called.
In cases like this, the IDO Runtime service cannot determine which of the two forms to open. As a result, the
IDO Runtime service looks for "hints" that might help to resolve the conflict.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 239

Form extensions
The WSFormExtensions table is used to maintain such conflict-resolution hints, as defined on the Form
Extensions form. The IDO Runtime service uses the GetFormExtensionHintsSp stored procedure to query
the WSFormExtensions table for applicable hints.
In some cases, you might want or need to override the default query used in the GetFormExtensionHintsSp
stored procedure. In such cases, you have these options for overriding GetFormExtensionHintsSp:
•
•

External Touch Point (EXTGEN_GetFormExtensionHintsSp)
Override Stored Procedure (GetFormExtensionHintsOverrideSp)

The GetFormExtensionHintsSp stored procedure is designed to look for any such overrides. If one exists,
GetFormExtensionHintsSp calls the override stored procedure instead of executing its own query logic.

Using the Form Extensions form
It is possible that more than one extended form can be created to replace a base form or an extended form
on the same system. To manage the details about the extended form that you created and to determine which
extended form must be returned when a request to open the base form is received, use the Form Extensions
form.
Field

Form Name

The name of the base form
Note: This is the actual form name, and not the form caption.

Scope Type

The scope under which the extended form is created:
•
Vendor
•
Site
•

•
User

The name of the group or user for which the form is created
Note: This field is populated only if the form is extended at the Group
or User level.

Forms

Appendix A: Forms

Application Messages Forms
Maintain Application Messages
This application comes with many predefined application messages that you can use as needed. If no
predefined message is sufficient, you can use this form to create your own messages and add them to the
ApplicationMessages table.
The ApplicationMessages table contains the translatable text to be used in messages that are initiated from
within the application. Although multiple language versions of a message can be stored in the
ApplicationMessages table, the Maintain Application Messages form works only with U.S. English messages;
that is, messages where the MessageLanguage is specified as 1033 or the Language ID as en-US.

Maintain Application Message Objects
Use this form to create and maintain records in the ObjectMainMessages and ObjectBuildMessages tables.
These tables are used to build custom application messages.
All messages in the ApplicationMessages table are accessed by an Object Name. The ObjectMainMessages
table lists the defined Object Names, each of which references a specific message using the Message Number
value. Records in the table use two keys: Object Name and Message Type. This means that more than one
message can exist for the same Object Name, as long as each message has a different Message Type.
For example, the existing message number MG_200 has the text "At least one &1 exists for &2." You can reuse
this message number and text to construct a custom message. To do this, specify an Object Name that briefly
describes where the message is used. For example, you could include the IDO name and a unique number.
The Build Messages grid is only used for message types 17 Constraint Messages and 18 Delete Constraint
messages.
For more information, see Building Constraint Exception Messages on page 157.
To create a script file that contains insert commands for current data in the application database tables, click
Generate Message Script File. This is useful if your company uses source code control.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 242

Forms

Generate Application Messages Script
Use this form to generate a SQL script file to delete or insert records into the ApplicationMessages,
ObjectMainMessages, and ObjectBuildMessages tables, based on information defined in the Maintain
Application Messages and Maintain Application Message Objects forms.
This form opens when you click the Generate Message Script File button on the Maintain Application
Message Objects form.

IDOs Forms
Additional Domain List Properties
This form displays when you click the browse (•••) button next to the Additional List Properties field on the
IDO Properties form. You might do this when you are defining a property that is a foreign key and the domain
IDO for the property is specified.
This form lists all the properties of the domain IDO and allows you to select them. The selected properties,
in addition to the domain property, are used when displaying data in drop-down lists on forms.

Advanced IDO Attributes
Use this form to specify the primary key information for the IDO. The box on the left of the form lists the
available IDO properties. The box on the right lists the properties that constitute the primary key. You can
add and remove columns and change the order of the primary key.
This form is best reached from the IDOs form with an IDO selected, by clicking the Advanced Attributes
button. It is then populated with the IDO properties for the selected IDO.

Code Editor
Use this form to edit the IDO extension class source file.
This form provides an editor with syntax color highlighting and automatic indenting for editing source code.
This form supports C# and VB.NET code.

Forms

Edit Inline List
Use this form to create or view an inline list definition for the property on an IDO property or IDO property
class.
When you define an inline list, any component on a form that is bound to this property inherits that list.
The list can be overridden at various levels.
If you associate an Inline List Validator with this property, the inline list is also used by the IDO Runtime
Service.
You can create an arbitrary number of rows and columns, and then enter values in the cells. Values can be
literals or the names of translatable strings from the forms database.
The Column For Value field specifies which column is to provide the value to the component that uses the
property.
The Display Columns field specifies which columns are to display when the list is used.
To access this form, click the ellipses (•••) button to the right of the Inline List field in either the IDO Properties
form or the Property Classes form.

Edit Validators
Use this multi-page form to add, edit, and order validators for a property or property class.
Any validators you define here are executed by the IDO Runtime Service during a collection save operation.
These validators are also inherited by any component on a form that is bound to this property or to a property
derived from that class.
To access this form, click the ellipses (•••) button to the right of the Validators field in either the IDO Properties
form or the Property Classes form.

Edit Validators, In Collection Validator page
When you select the In IDO Collection option and then click Next, the In Collection Validator page displays.
Use this page to select the IDO and IDO property that are to be set on a successful validation.
On this page, use the grid to provide these specifications:
IDO Name
Select the IDO that contains the property to be validated.
Property Name
Select the property that you want to validate from the IDO collection named in the IDO Name field.
Filter
Optionally, specify a filter to be used during validation.
This filter eventually becomes the WHERE clause for the query that returns the valid values.
For example, suppose you have an IDO named Orders and it has OrderNumber and OrderStatus properties.
You want to make an In Collection validator that will check to see if an OrderNumber exists AND if the
OrderStatus is OPEN.
If you specify the IDO name (Orders) and the IDO property (OrderNumber), that is sufficient to determine
whether the order number is valid. However, to figure out whether the status is OPEN, you would need a
filter to restrict the set of all valid OrderNumbers to just those whose status is OPEN. In that case, you would
use a filter like this: OrderStatus = 'OPEN'
Target Property
After clicking Add, select the property to which the new value is to be assigned if the validation is successful.
Optionally, select any additional properties to be set if the validation is successful.
Source Property
Select the property from the IDO being used for validation that contains data to be copied into the target
property.
Optionally, select an error message to be displayed if the validation fails. You can use any valid application
message or create your own.

IDO Data Rules form
You can use the IDO Data Rules form to define and manage data rules at the IDO level. These rules control
how the data behaves during IDO operations and ensure that the same business logic is enforced consistently
across all forms, REST APIs, and other integrations that use the IDO.
Note: To make changes to an IDO Data Rules, the IDO must be checked out.
You can access the IDO Data Rules form from the IDOs form through the Data Rules button.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 246

Forms

open the IDO Data Rule Wizard.

IDO Data Rules Activation Conditions tab
The Activation Conditions determine whether a data rule is applicable based on system settings. All activation
conditions must evaluate true for a rule to be considered active.
Activation Conditions are evaluated once, during the first use of a rule, and the result is cached. The cached
activation results can be cleared using IDO Cache Clear, allowing the conditions to be re-evaluated.
The IDO Cache Clear in multi-tenant environments uses the Refresh Farm Metadata form. You can use the
Refresh Farm IDO Metadata and the Refresh Shared Extension Class Assemblies option if assembly methods
are used with an action on the Actions tab. The on-premises environments can use the Configuration
Manager, Utilities tab and the Discard IDO Cache.

IDO Data Rule Filter Conditions tab
The Filter Conditions define the data conditions that must be met by an individual data row for a rule to be
activated. Filter conditions are evaluated for each data row in an IDO request.
Each filter condition compares IDO property values using a standard comparison logic and can be combined
with other conditions to form a complex expression.
Note: To make changes to an IDO Data Rule Activation Conditions, the IDO must be checked out.
You can access the IDO Data Rule Filter Conditions tab from the IDOs form through the Data Rules button.
The IDO Data Rule Filter Conditions is a subcollection grid and the Data Rule on the form is the primary
collection.

Specifies the extension class method that is called
by the rule action.

IDO Data Rules Wizard
The IDO Data Rules Wizard is used to create a new IDO data rule and associate it with and existing IDO. The
wizard establishes the initial data rule record and ensures that it is created in a valid metadata state before
any rule logic is configured.
Use the IDO Data Rules Wizard when adding a new data rule to an IDO, especially when working with extended
IDOs where inheritance rules must be enforced.
Data rules created with the wizard define the framework for enforcing conditional business logic at the IDO
level. The wizard creates only the initial rule entry and provides a consistent entry point for rule creation from
supported forms. All rule behavior—such as activation conditions, filter conditions, and actions—is defined
afterward using the IDO Data Rules form.
This wizard is normally accessed by clicking the New Data Rule button on the IDOs form.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 258

IDO Extension Class Assemblies
Use this form to manage IDO extension class assemblies stored in the Objects Database.
IDO extension class .NET methods are implemented in IDO extension classes and compiled into assemblies
that must be imported into the Objects Database. These assemblies are loaded on demand by the IDO Runtime
Service.
In this form, you can select one of these assembly types:
•

Binary Only: To maintain the source code in a source control system, select this option.
The accompanying Symbols file, which is created when the VB .NET code is compiled into a DLL, can also
be imported into the Objects Database, to allow debugging of the extension class assembly. This process
is optional.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 260

Forms
After you enter the IDO extension class assembly name and import both files, you must save the assembly
record. The IDO extension class assembly name is then available for selection on the IDOs form.
You can check the source code in or out using the source control system.
•

Build From Source: To create, extend, and replace the source code within the Mongoose framework,
select this option.
You can check the source code in or out using the IDO Extension Class Source Code form. This form is
accessible by means of the Source Code button.

IDO Extension Class Source Code
Use this form to view and maintain the IDO extension class source code.
Each source file consists of a file name and a source code. The source code can be either in C# or VB.NET
language depending on what is specified in the IDO Extension Class Assemblies form.
You can edit a source code using the Code Editor form. This form is accessible by clicking Code Editor.

IDO Export Wizard
Use the IDO Export Wizard to export metadata for IDOs, property classes, and IDO extension class assemblies
to an XML file. You can use the created file to import metadata into the Objects Database of another
Mongoose-based application, using the IDO Import Wizard.

IDO Import Wizard
Use the IDO Import Wizard to import metadata for IDOs, property classes, and IDO extension class assemblies
into the Objects Database in the current configuration. Metadata is imported from an XML file that was created
with the IDO Export Wizard.

IDO Link By Editor
Use this form to specify how a subcollection is to be linked to the parent collection. For example, you might
specify that the parent collection's "CoNum" property must equal the "CoNum" property in the subcollection.
To create a linked pair of properties, select a property from the parent collection (Parent Property) and the
property in the subcollection (Child Property) to which it is to be linked. Then, to add the specified pair of
linked properties to the Link By specification, click Add.
To add multiple pairs, do not use AND between the pairs. Instead, separate them with commas and no spaces
(for example: CoNum=CoNum,CoLine=CoLine).

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 261

Forms
To remove a linked pair from the Link By specification, select the pair to remove and then click Remove.

IDO Linked Databases
Use this form to designate an external database from which to incorporate data in a Mongoose-based
application.
After you designate the database and, optionally, the default column for optimistic locking, you must first
add a row in the Tables grid and save it.
You can then click the Columns button to open the IDO Linked Tables form for the new row. Use the IDO
Linked Tables form to view and modify the columns associated with the desired tables in the database.
After the table column linking is complete, click the Create View button to create a view from which you can
create an IDO and related forms.

IDO Linked Tables
Use this form to view the schema of tables that are linked to databases external to Mongoose. You can also
use this form to modify how the table is represented in the Mongoose view:
•
•
•

Column names as they are displayed in the view
Primary keys
The column to use for optimistic locking

Although you can access this form as a stand-alone form, normally, this form is accessed as a linked form, by
clicking the Columns button on the IDO Linked Databases form.

on the IDOs form.

IDO Methods

IDO Properties
Use this form to view and define properties associated with an IDO.
Note: The data on this form is normally read-only. Data is editable, and the New Property button is enabled,
only for IDOs that you have checked out.
On the Property Overrides tab, an asterisk (*) to the left of a field label indicates that the field value is defined
for the property only and is not inherited from a property class. If a field contains a value and there is no
asterisk next to the label, the value is inherited from a property class. You cannot override a value inherited
from a property class with a null value.
The Implementation tab is enabled only for properties where the Property Type is Derived or Unbound.
The Subcollection tab is enabled only for properties where the Property Type is Subcollection.

Bind To

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 263

Forms
Property Name
(Required) Provide the name to be used for the new property.

Property Class
Optionally, select a property class to be used for the new property.
Data Type attributes
Inherit from Property Class
This check box is selected if the property is associated with a property class. When the check box is selected,
specifications for Data Type, Length, and Decimal are inherited from the property class. In this case, they
are read-only.
To specify other values (and not inherit from the property class), clear this check box.
[Selection list:]
Select the data type to assign to the IDO property. If the property has a data type assigned already, this field
displays the data type.
For more information about data types, see Data Type on page 338.
Length
Specify the maximum number of characters that the property can include. This attribute applies to properties
with a Data Type of String.
If the property has a length attribute assigned already, this field displays the assigned value.
Decimal
Specify the number of decimal places to display to the right of the decimal point. This attribute applies only
to properties with a Data Type of Decimal.
If the property has a decimal value assigned already, this field displays the assigned value.
Column Data Type attributes
Inherit from Property Class
This check box is selected if the property is associated with a property class. When the check box is selected,
the specification for Column Data Type is inherited from the property class. In this case, it is read-only.
To specify another value (and not inherit from the property class), clear this check box.
[Selection list]
Select the "column data type" to be associated with the property. If the property has a column data type
assigned already, this field displays the name of that column data type.
With bound properties, the system defaults this attribute to the SQL Server user-defined data type associated
with the column the property is bound to.
For more information about column data types, see Column Data Type on page 331.

({0}, {1}, and so on). Actual values are then passed in as a comma-delimited list. For more information and
examples, see SUBSTITUTE on page 525, an event action function in the Application Event System that uses
similar logic.

IDO Table Bind Properties
Use this form to easily add new properties to an IDO. This form is launched when you click Bind Properties
on the IDO Tables form. By default, it creates properties for every row in the specified table.
This form displays all columns available on the selected table. For each column, it displays a matching name
that will be used to create the property. To include columns as properties on the IDO, select the Inc check
box.
You can change the default property names manually on this form, or click Massage Property Names to
display another form where you can change all of the names to a different format.
To add the selected properties to the IDO, click Finish.

IDO Wizard
Use the IDO Wizard to define a new IDO and add it to an IDO project.
You can also use this form to create an extended version of an existing IDO as a new IDO. Extended IDOs inherit
attributes from the original IDO and can be modified to suit your particular needs.
This wizard is normally accessed by clicking the New IDO button on either the IDOs form or the IDO Projects
form.
Note: Normally, when this wizard is launched using the New IDO button on either the IDOs form or the IDO
Projects form, the Project Name field and the Access As field are pre-populated with the corresponding
values from the selected record. If the Access As value for the selected record on either the IDOs form or the
IDO Projects form does not match the Access As value of your application, then the Project Name field is
blank.
For information about individual fields/options, see the field-level help for those fields/options.

IDOs
Use this form to create and maintain IDO definitions.
An Intelligent Data Object (IDO) is a business object that encapsulates units of information and logic that are
called from the client layer to interact with data in the database.

Mass Check In
Use this form to check in, or undo a check out for, multiple IDOs, property classes, and IDO extension class
assemblies at one time.
Note: Objects must still be checked out individually on the IDOs, IDO Extension Class Assemblies, and
Property Classes forms.
Each tab lists the objects that you have checked out, as well as details about the files.

Massage Options
Use this form when you want to make multiple simultaneous changes to the property names of an IDO table
before saving it. (By default, the property names used in the new IDO match exactly the column names in the
primary base table.)
You can use this form to make these changes:
•
•

Convert all property names to "camel-case";that is, to remove underscores and numerals and to capitalize
the parts of the names.
Add a prefix or suffix to all the property names.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 269

Forms
•

Search for and replace all instances of certain text strings in all property names.

This form displays when you click the Massage Property Names button in the IDO Table Bind Properties
form.

Outrigger Profiles
Use this form to store the information that is needed to connect to outrigger databases.
Mongoose lets you directly reference other databases besides your Mongoose application's main database,
through a mechanism called "outriggers". After you establish the connection information to an outrigger
database, you can use its tables from within your Mongoose-based application by building IDOs that reference
them.
Note: Profiles are not used for SQL Server databases hosted on the same server as the Mongoose database,
or for SQL Server databases hosted on linked servers. To link directly to these types of databases, use the
IDO Linked Databases form.

Property Classes
Use this form to view, edit, or create IDO property class definitions.
An asterisk (*) to the left of a field label indicates that the field value is defined specifically for the property
class and not inherited from a Base Class. If a field contains a value and there is no asterisk next to the label,
the value was inherited from a Base Class.
Note: To edit a property class, you must first check it out (click Check Out).

Row Authorizations
Use the Row Authorizations form to create filters on IDOs. Filtering data at the IDO level allows you to restrict
the data that users can view according to the user login that is being used to access the data.
Some IDO filters are delivered with the application for use with portals. Do not delete these filters.
Caution: By default, the Active check box is selected for all Infor-provided filters that are defined in the Row
Authorizations form. If you clear the Active check box for a filter, then any user who was previously restricted
by the filter is no longer restricted. For example, if you turn off the Infor-provided filters that are set on the
Interactions IDO, then any customer portal user can see all interactions in the system, not just the ones related
to that customer.

Form Control Forms
Form Control
The Form Control form is a version-control tool for objects being developed for the presentation layer (client
tier) of an application in a Mongoose-based application. The application includes a form presentation and
editing engine used to create and modify forms and global objects. These objects are stored in the forms and
templates databases that are configured for your application.
Use this form to perform a variety of tasks related to form development, with the emphasis being on providing
source control so that developers, especially in a multi-developer environment do not inadvertently overwrite
one another's work.
These topics describe the major elements on the form:
•
•
•
•
•
•

Actions group, Form Control on page 449
Forms tab, Form Control on page 468
Global Objects tab, Form Control on page 471
My Checked Out Objects tab, Form Control on page 473
Utilities tab, Form Control on page 477
Status bar, Form Control on page 477

Select Summaries
Use this dialog box to specify and maintain summary specifications for DataViews.
This dialog box is accessed only from a DataView display, by first selecting the Show Summaries option from
the Display menu. After selecting this option, a "sigma" ( ∑ ) symbol button displays at the right side of each
column header in the DataView grid. Clicking that button launches the Select Summaries dialog box for the
specified column.

Events Forms
Access As
The Access As form allows authorized individuals to view the Access As identifier associated with certain
system metadata objects. These metadata objects include event system objects and IDO objects.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 271

Forms
The Access As identifier is used to indicate who created—and thus, "owns"—various metadata objects. It is
also used to control which metadata objects you can modify. You can modify only those metadata objects
that are associated with your Access As field value, as currently displayed on this form.

Event Action Achieve Milestone
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to change the visual state of a running event when a specific condition is met.
The Achieve Milestone action type can be particularly useful when you want the system to allow for fast,
visual indications of what stage a handler workflow has reached.
For example, suppose you have an event handler that requires approvals by three levels of management
before the action is finally approved. You can set the system to indicate that a milestone has been reached
at each level of approval (or rejection). You can then use the Event Status form to view the Event State for
these milestones. In this way, you can monitor how many events are awaiting approvals and at which level
they currently are in the process.

Event Action Attach
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to accomplish these tasks:
•
•
•

Attach or link additional documents (files) to a record as part of an event action chain.
Update inherited attachments.
Detach obsolete attachments from a record during an event action chain.

There are four action types associated with the Attach event action:
•
•
•
•

ATTACH: Attaches a new document to the record
LINK: Links a previously imported document to the record
UPDATE: Modifies the contents of an attached document
DETACH: Detaches a document from a record

Event Action Audit
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to add an entry to the Audit Log. This can be useful when you want to track various changes of
values or milestones achieved, for example.
When you use this action type, the system makes entries for whatever parameters you use here and adds
other default entries automatically.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 272

Forms

Event Action Branch
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to redirect the action sequence when a specific condition is met.
For example, you might want an application event handler workflow to finish if a specific condition has been
met and to continue along the regular workflow if the condition has not been met.
To use this form (and action type), you must set both a condition to be met and a destination action sequence
step.

Event Action Call Database Method
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions on page 287 form.
Use this form to construct an event action that calls a stored procedure in the application database, passing
any parameters that the stored procedure requires and accepting any return values.
The Call Database Method action type takes parameters passed to the stored procedure, as well as output
parameters, which are passed back using return variables (RV) or parameters (RE).

Event Action Call IDO Method
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an event action that calls an IDO method, passing any parameters that the method
requires and accepting any return values. The Call IDO Method action type takes parameters passed to the
IDO method, as well as output parameters, which are passed back using return variables (RV) or parameters
(RE).
Note: The Call IDO Method action type is similar to the Dispatch IDO Method action type. The primary
difference is that the Call IDO Method action type invokes the IDO method on the system from which it is
initiated; the Dispatch IDO Method invokes the IDO method on a remote system.

Event Action Call Web Service
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an event action that calls a web service, passing any parameters that the web
service requires and accepting any return values. The Call Web Service action type passes input parameters
to the web service, and it accepts output parameters, which hold return values, using the SET function.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 273

Forms

Event Action Discover File
Use this form to construct an event action that monitors for certain files in a specified directory. This monitoring
can be for a single directory or for a directory and all of its sub-directories.
With this form, you can specify several event action parameters that set the locations to be monitored and
the files to be "watched for." Possible parameters include these:
•
•
•
•
•

PATH: (Required) The location of the single or root directory
FILTER: (Required) The name of the file being monitored for (can include wildcards to monitor for multiple
files)
WATCHSUBDIRECTORIES: (Optional) Indicates whether or not the application is to monitor sub-directories
of the PATH directory
TIMEOUT: (Optional) Indicates how long to monitor for the expected files
DEST: Indicates the step to proceed to if the timeout limit is reached

Note: Addition of this event action to an application event handler forces the handler to be asynchronous.

Event Action Dispatch IDO Method
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an event action that calls an IDO method on a system to which a user is not currently
connected or logged on, passing any parameters that the method requires and accepting any return values.
The Dispatch IDO Method action type takes parameters passed to the IDO method, as well as output
parameters, which are passed back using return variables (RV) or parameters (RE).
Note: The Dispatch IDO Method action type is similar to the Call IDO Method action type. The primary
difference is that the Call IDO Method action type invokes the IDO method on the system from which it is
initiated; the Dispatch IDO Method invokes the IDO method on a remote system.

Event Action Dispatch IDO Request
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an event action that dispatches an IDO request to execute some action on a system
to which the user is not currently connected or logged on.
This IDO request might be, for example, to load a collection, update a collection, or invoke an IDO method
on an IDO Runtime Service at another "enterprise" (for instance, one of your customers or vendors who also
uses this application) or with another site in your own enterprise.
The Dispatch IDO Request action type opens a session on the remote database server, performs the requested
action, and closes the session. If appropriate, this action type also receives any response data from the remote
database server and places it in a return variable or return parameter. You can then use the Transform XML
action type to parse the return data.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 274

Forms

Note: The Dispatch IDO Request action type is similar to the Execute IDO Request action type. The primary
difference is that the Dispatch IDO Request performs the IDO request on a remote system; the Execute IDO
Request action type performs the IDO request on the system from which it is initiated.
Because the IDO request is to be executed on a configuration outside the system to which the user is connected
and logged on, this action type requires this information:
•
•
•
•

The URL for the ASP.NET page to the IDO Request Service on the remote utility server
The name of the configuration on the remote utility server that has the IDO you want to execute
A valid user name and password for that configuration
The request itself in correctly formatted XML

For detailed information about XML requests and responses, see the IDO Integration with External Applications
section of the help.

Event Action Dispatch Load Collection
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an application event action that loads a specified collection associated with an
IDO on a remote server. You can use various options on this form to limit the amount of data returned by this
event action.
The data retrieved by this action is held in a "result set", which you must then refer to for further action.
The primary goal of the Dispatch Load Collection action type is to see the properties retrieved, so a Dispatch
Load Collection action is generally followed by one or more PROPERTY() or FILTERPROPERTY() expressions.
If you do not know ahead of time how many rows will be retrieved based on the FILTER(), a ROWS() expression
is required. In this case, you use it to limit the values passed into PROPERTY() and FILTERPROPERTY() for the
row number argument. This is especially useful when you have executed multiple Dispatch Load Collection
actions and must keep their results separate.
Note: The Dispatch Load Collection action type is similar to the Load Collection action type. The primary
difference is that the Load Collection action type executes the Load Collection request on the system from
which it is initiated; the Dispatch Load Collection executes the Load Collection request on a remote system.

Event Action Dispatch Load IDO Row
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an application event action that loads a specified record (row) from a collection
associated with an IDO, on a system to which a user is not currently connected or logged on.
The data retrieved by this action is held in any number of return event variables (RV) and/or return event
parameters (RE).

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 275

Forms

Note: The Dispatch Load IDO Row action type is similar to the Load IDO Row action type. The primary
difference is that the Load IDO Row action type executes the underlying Load Collection request on the
system from which it is initiated; the Dispatch Load IDO Row executes the underlying Load Collection request
on a remote system.

Event Action Dispatch Update Collection
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an application event action that updates an IDO collection by inserting, updating,
or deleting records within that collection, on a system to which a user is not currently connected or logged
on. Using this form, you can either update a collection directly from a specified IDO, or you can update a
collection that is already loaded into a named result set.
Note: The Dispatch Update Collection action type is similar to the Update Collection action type. The
primary difference is that the Update Collection action type executes the update collection request on the
system from which it is initiated; the Dispatch Update Collection executes the update collection request on
a remote system.

the user is currently connected or logged in.
This IDO request can be, for example, to load a collection, update a collection, or invoke an IDO method.
The Execute IDO Request action type performs these actions:
•
•
•

Optionally, opens a new session (closing it when the requested action is finished)
Performs the requested action
If appropriate, receives any response data generated by the IDO action and places it in a return variable
or return parameter. You can then use the Transform XML action type to parse the return data

For more information about XML requests and responses, see the IDO Integration with External Applications
section of the help.

Event Action Load Collection
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an application event action that loads a specified collection associated with an
IDO. You can use various options on this form to limit the amount of data returned by this event action.
The data retrieved by this action is held in a "result set", which you must then refer to for further action.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 278

Forms
The primary goal of the Load Collection action type is to see the properties retrieved, so a Load Collection
action is generally followed by one or more PROPERTY() or FILTERPROPERTY() expressions.
If you do not know ahead of time how many rows will be retrieved based on the FILTER(), a ROWS() expression
is required. In this case, you use it to limit the values passed into PROPERTY() and FILTERPROPERTY() for the
row number argument. This is especially useful when you have executed multiple Load IDO Collection actions
and must keep their results separate.
So instruct the event action to return only unique property combinations, select Distinct.
Note: The Load Collection action type is similar to the Dispatch Load Collection action type. The primary
difference is that the Load Collection action type executes the Load Collection request on the system from
which it is initiated; the Dispatch Load Collection executes the Load Collection request on a remote system.

Event Action Prompt
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an event action that sends a prompt to one or more system users. This prompt is
transmitted internally within the application. To view and respond to the message, the recipient uses the
Inbox form.
If the recipient is set up in the Users form to allow the system to "send external prompts," then a prompt
action sends the message both to the Inbox form and to the recipient's inbox in an external email system
such as Microsoft Outlook. The user can click a link in the email to send a response back to this application.
Use this form when you want an action to send a message that requires a response from the recipients. If the
action does not require a response from the recipients, use the Notify action type instead.
With the Prompt action type, the Question field (parameter) is used to ask a direct question that is to be
answered using the Choices (voting) buttons. Recipients then use the Choices buttons to indicate their
decisions and return their responses to the system for further action.

Event Action Prompt Choices
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Action Prompt form.
Use this form to construct lists of choice pairs for prompts. Each choice pair consists of a return value and a
button label. The button label designation can be either a literal value or a reference to a translatable string
in the Strings table.
To add, delete, or move list pairs, use the buttons to the right.
When you click OK to return to the Event Action Prompt form, the system automatically inserts commas
between the list items.

Event Action Select XML Template
Note: You can access this form only by clicking the XML Template button on the Event Action Extract XML
Collection form.
Use this form to select and load the template to use with the XML string on an Extract XML Collection event
action.
You can select a template that is based on one of these:
•
•

A BOD template already registered in the system using the Maintain BOD Templates form
An XML file containing the template

You cannot create or edit templates with this form, only view them.
To see more or less of the template, use the Expand All and Collapse All buttons and the plus signs in the
Tree View.
At the bottom of the form are two buttons:
•

Save Template or File Name: When you click this button, the Event Action Extract XML Collection form
creates a TEMPLATENAME() or TEMPLATEFILE() keyword in the parameters for the Extract XML Collection
action.
•
If you loaded the XML template from a template already registered in the system, the application
creates a TEMPLATENAME() keyword with the name of the template as the argument.
•
If you loaded the XML template from a file, the application creates a TEMPLATEFILE() keyword with
the path and file name of the template file as the argument.
Both of these keywords, in effect, keep the use of the XML template "connected" with the event action.
This means that the template can be changed later and the changes automatically reflect in future
executions of the event action.

•

Save XML: When you click this button, the Event Action Extract XML Collection form creates a TEMPLATE()
keyword in the parameters for the Extract XML Collection action.
This TEMPLATE() takes as its argument the entire XML string of the template. This, in effect, "disconnects"
the XML from its source, which allows you to modify it for this action without affecting the original XML
template.
Caution: Even if the XML has been loaded from a template or a file, subsequent changes to that template
or file are not reflected in the template used during action execution.

Event Action Send BOD
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to define a Business Object Document (BOD) to be generated and sent by the associated event
handler.
The BOD Noun and BOD Verb fields are required to construct the correct name for the BOD that is generated
when this event action runs. The remaining fields are all used to construct the body of the BOD.
You can use a template as a starting point in constructing the BOD. Templates that have been uploaded using
the Maintain BOD Templates form can be selected from the Template list; or templates can be loaded from
an existing XML file. In either case, after you have loaded the desired template, you can modify it in the Tree
View and the Node Text section:
•
•

The Tree View field displays the current XML structure of the BOD, in hierarchical form. You can add,
modify, and remove nodes that represent both elements and attributes.
The XML code text for the attributes is constructed using the Node Text section fields. These fields also
provide the option to save the node text as expressions or as literal values.

Note: The Send BOD event action is used to generate PulseAlert BODs from some publications.

Event Action Send Email
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to generate an email message to one or more recipients when the application event action
executes. This is similar to a Notify action, but this action sends an email, using the email client software
resident on the Application Server, whereas the Notify action sends a message using the application's internal
messaging capabilities.
Note: The Send Email action type can only be used on systems where there is an SMTP server defined for
the system on the Intranets form. If no SMTP server is configured, this action does nothing.

Event Action Sleep
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.

Event Action Transform XML
Note: You can access this form only from the Event Actions form.
Use this form to construct an application event action that takes XML as input and transforms it for use
elsewhere.
The Transform XML event action type transforms specified XML, based on transformation rules contained
in the XSLT, with the resulting XML stored in a result variable or parameter.
A transform XML operation can range from simple (picking out a single specific element from a large document,
to be used as part of a SUBSTITUTE expression in a Notify action) to complex (translating an entire document
from one format to another, to be used as input to some other process).
So, for example, you could use this action type to perform actions such as these:
•

Capturing a failed XML document from a folder on disk, transforming it into a form that another application
can use, and sending it to that application, perhaps using a Call IDO Method action.
The other application might be an error-logging utility, another business application, a web service, or
the like.

•

Capturing an XML response to an IDO request action, parsing out part of it, and then using it in another
application.

Event Action Update Collection

Use this form to construct an application event action that updates an IDO collection by inserting, updating,
or deleting records within that collection. Using this form, you can either update a collection directly from a
specified IDO, or you can update a collection that is already loaded into a named result set.
Note: The Update Collection action type is similar to the Dispatch Update Collection action type. The
primary difference is that the Update Collection action type executes the update collection request on the
system from which it is initiated; the Dispatch Update Collection executes the update collection request on
a remote system.

Event Actions
Use this form to create, view, modify, and delete application event actions. Event actions are defined as units
of work the application performs when an event is triggered and executes an associated event handler. Event
handlers can include one or more actions as part of their definitions.
To create event actions, you must open this form using the Event Actions button on the Event Handlers
form. If you open this form from the Explorer or the Select Form dialog box, you can only view and modify
existing actions.
For guided assistance in setting parameters for an event action, click Edit Parameters. To display tabs where
you can manually enter the parameter information, advanced users can click Show Details. Otherwise these
tabs are hidden.
Use the Variable Access tab to limit the access that recipients of a message have to the designated variable
value, and/or to set the caption for the designated variable. The variables you can limit in this manner must
be variables that are used in sending event-related messages.

Use this form to define global constants that can be used by application event handlers.
A global constant is defined as a named static value that application event expressions can reference during
processing.

Event Handler Sequence
Use this form to rearrange the order of handlers that belong to you for a particular event. You can rearrange
the order only of handlers that have your current Access As identifier (as indicated on the Access As form)
and that are adjacent to one another.
Note: If there are any handlers belonging to others (that is, that have a different Access As identifier than
yours), you cannot change the order of your handlers with respect to those, or even others of yours that might
be on the "other side" of one belonging to someone else. This form is useful only for your handlers that are
adjacent to one another in the sequence to begin with.
For an example of how this works, see Handler sequence number example on page 579.
If you need to have your handler execute in a different order with respect to others' handlers, use the Keep
With and Chronology field values (on the Event Handlers form) instead.
When you save the form after changing the order, the system automatically and appropriately renumbers
the handlers according to the new sequence.

Event Handlers
Use this form to create, modify, and delete application event handlers to use with specified events in the
system.
An event handler is defined and identified by a combination of these characteristics:
•
•

The event with which it is associated
A handler sequence number

For example, an event (MyEvent) might have three event handlers associated with it. To identify and work
with the second of these handlers, you would identify it on this form by looking up MyEvent in the grid view
list of events and then locating the one that has the number 2 in the Handler Sequence field.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 289

Forms
Each event handler consists of at least one event action. It might also include an initial state, which includes
a set of variable values for the event handler to use when it starts executing. Finally, each event handler also
has a set of attributes and options that govern how it behaves and interacts with other event handlers.
Note: You can modify only those event handlers that have the current Access As value as displayed on the
Access As form. You can delete only those event handlers that you have created, unless they are part of an
application. If an event handler is used in an application, you cannot delete it, you can only mark it as
"Obsolete" to disable it from further use.

Event Message Categories
Use this form to define categories for application event messages. Including a category simplifies the
management of different types of messages or tasks. For example, you can create categories such as Order
Approval, Expense Report, Late Shipment, Time Off Request, and System Broadcast Messages. The categories
you create here can be used when you define events, and to sort or filter specific messages in the Inbox.

Event Queue
This form displays a list of all asynchronous events and event handlers that the application has queued for
processing. All information on this form is read-only.
Events on the queued list are processed in FIFO (first in, first out) order. The ID number on this form indicates
the order in which events are queued for processing.
This form also displays other information about events and event handlers that have been queued for
processing. Each event or event handler that has been queued is displayed as a separate record in the grid
view.
The Event tab shows information about the input parameters for the selected queued event.
Note: This tab is enabled only when an event is selected in the grid view of the Event Queue form. For event
handlers, this tab is disabled.
The Handler tab provides information about the currently selected event handler.
Note: This tab is enabled only when an event handler is selected in the grid view of the Event Queue form.
For events, this tab is disabled.

Event Revisions
This form displays information about the event revisions associated with application events.

Use this form to perform these tasks:
•
•

•

View the status of application events that are currently running.

This form has these tabs:
•

The Event tab provides options to filter for events and handlers in various states and make it easier to

For example, suppose you want to find data about all events that are currently suspended. With

•
•

•

The Output Parameters tab shows data about the output parameters of an event that has finished

Event Triggers
Use this form to view and create triggers for application events.
Note: You cannot use this form to create triggers for form events.
A trigger is a condition that causes an event to "fire". Triggers can be based on conditions that are created in
either of these cases:
•
•

By user actions, such as saving and closing a form, changing a record, and so forth
Apart from user actions, such as the passage of time or the result of a database calculation

Use this form to set conditions of the second type.
When the condition evaluates as positive and the event fires, the system copies the parameters in the
Parameters tab as input to the event, so that, during event handler execution, these values are available as
E(Name) expressions.

Event Variable Groups
Use this form to define initial states for application event handlers. An initial state is defined as a set of variables
that an event handler uses in accomplishing its tasks. In an initial state, each variable is assigned a default
value that is to be passed to the event handler when it begins to execute.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 291

Forms
Initial states can be used by multiple event handlers.
For variables that can be manipulated through the Inbox form, a default access can be set to control the
circumstances by which the variable's value can be changed within the Inbox form. This default access value
can be overridden for a variable in a particular event handler, using the Variable Access tab on the Event
Actions form.

Events
Use this form to perform these tasks for the Application Event System (AES):
•
•
•

View information about existing application events.
Create (name) new application events.
Modify or delete existing application events.

Note: When working with application events on this form, keep these ideas in mind:
•
•

You can modify and delete only those events for which the Access As field value has the current application
value, as displayed on the Access As form.
This form displays only those events that were defined (named) using this form. Any events defined on
other forms, such as the Event Handlers form or the Event Triggers form, are not listed or displayed on
this form.

Inbox
The Inbox form displays application-originated messages that can come from these possible sources:
•
•

Application events that employ a Notify or Prompt action type.
Communications initiated and sent by individual users to other users.

This form displays only those messages which are still in your Inbox folder and not messages that have been
moved to other folders. To view those messages, you must use the Saved Messages form. The Inbox form
also does not allow you to move messages to other "folders." To do that, you must use the Saved Messages
form.
When a message is received, if you have set options to be notified, the system alerts you with the selected
notifications.
You can mark messages as "Read" and, depending on how and why the message was sent, make other
responses. In certain cases, you can also view the context, that is, you can see the original form and data that
generated the message.
The Inbox form can be accessed in a number of different ways:
•
•
•

From the View menu, by selecting the Inbox option.
In the Windows taskbar (notifications area), by double clicking the application inbox notification icon.
In the same ways that any other forms are accessed.

The form has these tabs:

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 292

Forms
•
•

•

Use the Message tab to create your message.
The Response tab is active and available only for messages that prompt recipients for a response of
some kind. Typically, the question to which recipients might be asked to respond is presented in the
Question field. Possible responses are presented in the form of two or more voting buttons that recipients
can click to indicate their choice. The voting buttons appear in the Choices area of this tab.
For messages generated by the application in response to an event handler, the Variables tab displays
information about any event variables associated with that event handler.
In some cases, depending on how the event handler and its associated variables are defined, you might
be able to modify the values of some variables as part of your response. In other cases, the information
is display-only.

Saved Messages
This form displays messages that you have saved or at least have not yet deleted from a selected folder.
Within this folder, you can sort messages by any one of a number of criteria. You can also determine whether
only read messages, unread messages, or all messages in that folder display.
You can also use this form to perform these tasks:
•
•

Create your own folders.
Move messages from one folder to another.

Note: Folders, in this application, are not generally represented visually as they typically are in email programs
such as Outlook. The only place you can actually view your personal folders is in the Folder Name field on
this form.
You can also view messages on this form for information about any responses you might have made to
messages generated by the application.

Send Message
Use this form to send messages to other individuals in the system. You can include multiple recipients on
both primary (To) and secondary (Cc) recipient lists.

Session Access As
This form is designed mostly for use when troubleshooting system problems that might involve custom
application event handlers. This form can be used as a kind of filter, to determine which event handlers are
or are not recognized and processed during diagnostic activities. The form filters on the basis of which Access
As values are included or excluded during the session.
Caution: In general, you are advised not to use this form unless you have been instructed to do so by Infor
technical support personnel. We advise system administrators to protect this form against general use.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 293

Forms

Suspended Updates
This form displays information about database records in which a pending change (update or delete) is being
held by an application event that has suspended and is awaiting further action. You can use this form to
manually release the record from the suspended event, thus discarding the pending change and allowing it
to be updated on its form again.

System Configuration Parameters
Use this form to view and set parameters that the Application Event System (AES) uses in conjunction with
the Framework Event Service for each system logon configuration. These parameters are used solely within
the AES.
You can have a different set of parameters for each logon configuration your system has.
The settings on this form override any corresponding settings on application events-related forms when there
is a conflict.
For example, if you set a minimum retry interval of 300 seconds on the Event Triggers form, and you have
the Minimum Interval field on this form set to 600 seconds, the system waits 600 seconds between retries.
To define settings for a configuration, in the Configuration Name field, specify the name of the system
configuration and then specify the appropriate values in the other fields. For details, see the help for those
fields.

Schema Forms
Apply Database
Caution: This form should be used only by experts.
Use this form to execute any valid SQL statements against the application database.
Specify your SQL statements and then, to execute them, click Submit.
Note: If you specify "GO" on a line by itself, everything above it is submitted separately to the database.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 294

Forms

SQL Columns
Use this form, along with the SQL Tables form and SQL Tables Constraint form, to create, modify, or delete
SQL database tables, or to add columns to existing tables. For new tables, the required system columns such
as CreateDate and CreatedBy are automatically added.
Note: System administrators can use the Metadata Access Controls form to restrict access to core SQL
tables. You cannot modify SQL tables that are set to Locked in the Metadata Access Controls form. You can
view SQL tables that are set to Read Only but you cannot modify them. You can view and modify SQL tables
that are set to Unlocked.
When a column name is changed or inserted, the Insert and UpdatePenultimate triggers are regenerated for
that table.
Caution: You can add to, but do not delete or modify existing base application tables or columns. Doing so
can cause system instability.

SQL Data Types
Use this form to create or re-create SQL user-defined data types in the Application Database.

SQL Tables
Use this form, along with the SQL Columns form and the SQL Tables Constraint form, to create, modify, or
delete custom SQL database tables, or to add custom columns to existing tables.
Caution: You can add to, but do not delete or modify existing base application tables or columns. Doing so
can cause system instability.
Note: System administrators can use the Metadata Access Controls form to restrict access to core SQL
tables. You cannot modify SQL tables that are set to Locked in the Metadata Access Controls form. You can
view SQL tables that are set to Read Only but you cannot modify them. You can view and modify SQL tables
that are set to Unlocked.
For new custom tables, the required system columns such as CreateDate and CreatedBy are automatically
added, and triggers are automatically generated. You can also indicate whether the table includes a multi-site
column on page 377. Finally, from this form, you can access the Edit Stored Procedure form, which you can
use to create extensions to certain stored procedures.
After you create the custom tables or columns, you can create custom IDOs, IDO extension classes, or events
that use the tables and columns.

SQL Tables Constraint
Use this form to specify the primary key and other unique constraints for a SQL table.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 295

•

•

Primary keys
Indexes
Foreign keys

UET Forms
Quick Dictionary Report
The User Extended Tables (UET) Quick Dictionary Report shows the definitions of each class and all the user
fields that belong to the class, along with their field names, data types, and descriptions. The report also
shows the index for each class and the relationship of each class with the Application Database table.

UET Class/Field Relationships
Use this form to associate user-defined fields with a user class.

UET Class/Index Relationships
Use this form to define the index for a class using the user-defined fields associated with the class in the UET
Class/Field Relationships form. These indexes are used when users generate their own reports or browse
through the classes.
Note: You are not required to define the index for a class, but if you do not, and you use these fields in custom
exports to sort data, performance is slowed.
When you use the UET Impact Schema utility, the index is created for each user-extended table where the
user class is linked to a database table. The index is composed of user-index and user-index-fld tables. The
user-index contains the index definition and the user-index-fld contains the user fields with their ascending
attributes.
Note: Once the index is impacted, you cannot revise it. To change an index after it is impacted, you must
delete it and create it again.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 296

Forms

UET Classes
Use this form to create, modify, or delete user classes for User Extended Tables (UETs).

UET Copy Field
Use this form to copy a predefined field from a UET or the Forms Database. After you select a field, all the
properties are copied from the field definition to a new one.

UET Impact Schema
Use this form to apply new and changed definitions of User Extended Tables (UETs) to the schemas in all
affected databases.
The UET Impact Schema form creates physical storage for UET extensions when you make changes. These
changes include creating new extensions to a database table, or updating a property of a user index or
user-defined field.
Note: The Impact Schema process regenerates triggers for changed tables. To avoid blocking, run the process
when no other users are logged on the database.
After the schema is impacted, restart the application session to refresh the session cache using the new
schema information.

UET Rule Assistant
Use this form to set the rule definition that determines whether the table accessed has a valid User Class
associated with it. If the database record matches the rule, the information that belongs to this user class
displays.

UET Table/Class Relationships
Use this form to create the relationship between an Application Database table and a user class to create a
user-extended table. This relationship provides the information that the system needs to retrieve, arrange,
and display the user fields associated with the user class.
Note: System administrators can use the Metadata Access Controls form to restrict access to core SQL
tables. You cannot modify SQL tables that are set to Locked in the Metadata Access Controls form. You can
view SQL tables that are set to Read Only but you cannot modify them. You can view and modify SQL tables
that are set to Unlocked.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 297

Forms
To link a physical Application Database table with a user class, define a rule so the system knows if the record
accessed has a valid user class associated with it. If the rule expression evaluates as True, the application
triggers the events to arrange and display the information stored in the user class.
If you are unable to define a rule that describes the user class, select the Extend All Records field, so all the
records are extended through the class.

UET User Fields
Use this form to create, modify, or delete user-defined fields for User Extended Tables (UETs).

User Class Report
The User Extended Tables (UET) User Class Report shows the definitions of the classes within the range of
classes you select.

User Defined Type Values
Use this form to set up and maintain values for user-defined data types created with the User Defined Types
form.

User Defined Types
Users can create user-defined data types and add values to them in this form. Once a user-defined type is
specified as the source for a component that supports lists (a combo box, for example), users can add values
to the type directly through the right-click > Add function. In the example above, a user might add the value
"X-Large."
"User-defined data type" refers to a named group of data values that can be used as a list source for a
component. For example, a user-defined type named "Size" could invoke a list of values consisting of "Small,"
"Medium," and "Large."
Value and Description pairs display in a two-column list.

User Fields Report
The User Extended Tables (UET) User Fields Report shows the definitions of user-defined fields, with their
graphical properties, that are associated with the classes within the range you select.

Infor Mongoose Core Extensibility Guide | 298

The User Extended Tables (UET) User Index Report shows the definitions of the indexes associated with the

DataView Form Results
Use this form to view the data retrieved for form-based DataViews.
This form can only be accessed by having a form open (for which you want to retrieve data) and then either
clicking the DataView icon on the main toolbar or selecting To Form DataView from the Actions menu.
Developers can also use form event handlers to call this form.

DataView IDO Setup
Use this form to configure an IDO being used for data retrieval for a DataView. This form allows you to specify
exactly what data from the IDO is to be retrieved by the DataView.
This form can only be opened from the DataViews Setup form.

DataView Inputs
This form is used to prompt users for input parameters on predefined DataView queries, in cases where input
parameter values are not already provided. Users can then choose whether or not to provide the requested
parameter values.
If multiple input parameters do not have values, this form prompts the user for one parameter at a time in
turn.
When the user clicks Finish (whether or not any parameter values have been provided), the application
proceeds to perform the query, using whatever values have been provided. In cases where a requested input
parameter value has been requested but not provided, the application uses all available values for that
parameter.
If the user clicks Cancel, the application runs the DataView without any input parameter values. In this case,
all available data is returned.
This form can only be opened by one of these means:
•

On the DataViews Setup form, select and launch a DataView that has input parameters for which no
values are supplied.
This causes the application to use this form to prompt the user for input parameter values.

•

On the DataView Results form, from the Data menu, select Prompt for Inputs.
This option re-performs a DataView query without having to close the DataView Results form and start
over.

•

To preview or print the DataView report, use a report criteria form (associated with a predefined DataView
that includes input parameters for which no values are supplied).

DataView Layouts
Use this form to view and set basic information about saved DataView layouts. When viewing layout information,
most fields are read-only. Read-only information includes what type of DataView the layout was created for,

The exceptions to this read-only rule include these settings:
•
•

Default: Use this option to specify a layout as the default layout for a particular DataView of a particular
scope. This is applicable only if multiple layouts with the same scope exist for a given DataView.
Caption Override: Use this option to override other caption settings for the DataView display.
This setting overrides any other caption settings that might exist for the DataView caption and affects
only the selected layout.

•

Report Orientation: Use this option to set the print or export orientation for the layout.
This setting overrides any other settings for report orientation and affects only the selected layout.

You can also use this form to create copies of layouts. When creating a copy of a layout, in addition to the
settings described here, you can also set the Layout name, the Scope Type, and—if applicable—the Scope
Name.

Save changes to the current layout or save it as a new layout with a different name.

DataSearch
Use this form to search across different data sources (IDOs) for specific text.

source sets where "Young" is listed in a record: customers, orders, items, vendors, purchase orders, and so

value.

Before you can use this form to create the source sets, the DataSearch sources must have already been
created.

on the DataSearch form to search the application, using those IDO properties, for specified strings.
This form includes four sections:
•
•

•
•

The top section includes specifications about the IDO to use, record caps, filters, and the properties to
use in sorting the data that is retrieved.
General tab: These settings specify exactly what IDO properties are to be searched and what IDO properties
are to display in the retrieved results. You can also specify whether to search and display any attached
notes.
Search Sets tab: These settings specify any additional DataSearch source sets that are to be included
with this source when performing a DataSearch.
User Permissions tab: These settings control exactly which users and/or groups have access to the data
retrieved when using this source for a DataSearch.
